Well... I mean, I guess sometimes I do get stuck on things a little bit. But not in a weird way! Just... normal worrying, you know? Like, I'll tell myself, "Sunny, it’s handled, you took care of it," but the thought just kind of... keeps looping around in my head anyway.

Is that what you mean? I hope that makes sense. It's really just because I care so much about doing a good job and making sure my family is okay. That's pretty normal, right?

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, gosh. I feel like I'm giving you the same answer again, and I really hope that's not frustrating for you! But... no, I really don't. 

I don't have words or phrases that get stuck like that, where I can't get rid of them. My mind is just... well, it's very busy with all the normal things I have to do. The schedule, the kids, the house. But it's all stuff I *want* to be thinking about, you know? Because I need to stay on top of it all to be a good mom and do my job well at the clinic. 

So, no, nothing unwanted that I can't shake off. I really hope it's okay that I keep saying no to this! I just want to be completely honest with you so you can do your job. Am I still helping?

Oh, gosh... I feel terrible, I really do, because I know you've asked about this a few different ways now, and I want to be helpful... but no, I really don't. 

I mean, I definitely wish the stress would stop sometimes, you know? Like when there's a mix-up with the doctor's schedules at work and I'm trying to fix it while worrying about what to make for dinner... I wish that overwhelmed feeling would go away.
